One writer per worktree or branch (principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state). Prefer a verifier on a different observed model family. Inherit the parent selection unless explicit supported selection is observed. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified; an unavailable required family blocks that gate.\\n\\nDepth stays at coordinator, track, worker. Author the track decomposition per project (build, landing, and verification are common cuts, not a required shape). Hard-coded swarm trees were tried and parked as too rigid.\\n\\n#### Store layout\\n\\nChoose an explicitly authorized task-owned writable `orchestrate/<project-slug>/` outside the package tree. Every file has exactly one writer. Owners publish facts, readers aggregate at read time. Use `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>`, written below as `orch`. `ORCH_STORE` is the alternative explicit binding. Plain TSV and JSON stay readable without the CLI.\\n\\n- `preferences.md` is the standing-orders register: numbered lines, one constraint each (model policy, stack shape and count, verification bar, forbidden paths, escalation policy). Paste it verbatim into every spawn and every resume. Directives decay across resumes, and each dropped one costs a human turn. When you catch yourself restating an instruction, append the line before you act (principle-encode-lessons-in-structure).\\n- `overview.md` is the durable PR and issue DB. Append. Never rewrite wholesale per event.\\n- `units.tsv` has one row per unit: id, track, state, branch, PR, head SHA, brief path. Update rows in place.\\n- `frontier.json` is the computed merge frontier, per Stack safety.\\n- `ledger.tsv` is the verification ledger, per Verification.\\n- `inbox/` holds completion pointers. `gates.md` parks human gates (question, options, default on no answer).\\n- `decisions.tsv` is the trail via the show-me-your-work skill.\\n- `status.md` is derived from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv` at each drain, never hand-maintained. Regenerate it from the tables instead of narrating events into it.\\n\\n\\n#### Helper contract\\n\\nRead [orch CLI](../scripts/orch/orch.ts), [store](../scripts/orch/store.ts), [tests](../scripts/orch/orch.test.ts), [bootstrap](../scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](../scripts/package.json) and [lock](../scripts/bun.lock). The CLI requires the exact pinned dependencies in a separately authorized writable copy. The operative package has no installed dependencies; the tested helper snapshot is evidence for that snapshot only. No automatic install occurs. Read docs/testing/helper-setup.md for the exact-copy validation boundary and retained historical setup evidence. It grants no installation approval and transfers no historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh explicit authorization for the exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache under Herd. Missing dependencies block execution.\\n\\nUse these actual commands through the explicit `orch` binding:\\n\\n- `init`; `standing add <line>`; `standing show --json`. Compact standing output truncates after four rows, so use JSON or read preferences.md to obtain every standing order.\\n- `unit add <id> --track <track> --brief <path>`; `unit set <id> --state <state> --branch <branch> --pr <number> --sha <full-head-sha>`; `unit get <id>`; `unit list --json`.\\n- `ledger record <pr> <full-head-sha> <verdict> --evidence <path> --verifier <identity>`; `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>`; `ledger summary`. A missing exact key returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2. Usage or runtime failure returns exit 1; success returns 0.\\n- `inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> --report <path>`; `inbox drain --peek --json`; `inbox drain --json`; `inbox count`. Peek before drain, persist that receipt to an owned artifact and read it back. Capture the complete actual drain output to another owned receipt and verify readback before classifying pointers. Drain removes the queued pointers; arrivals between peek and drain can change the actual batch. If the receipt is lost or interruption makes consumption uncertain, reconcile actual artifacts before retrying or updating rows.\\n- `gate park <id> --question <question> --options <options> --default <answer>`; `gate list --json`; `gate resolve <id> --answer <answer>`. Resolve permission gates only from actual authorization.\\n- `frontier set --repo <stack-owner-clone>`, optionally `--prs <bottom-to-top-numbers>`; `frontier show --json`; `status`. Frontier discovery runs local git and gt inspection against that clone. Missing gt metadata, malformed output or pin mismatch is an error, never guessed frontier state.\\n\\nThe helper does not spawn, wait, wake, enforce worker ownership, validate full revision syntax for unit or ledger input, inspect evidence paths, enforce terminal states or verify permissions. Keep immutable assignments and append-only attempts separately under the assignment contract. Match identity, input revision, full current head SHA, artifacts and observed method before accepting results. Ledger counts include historical revisions; filter against the current unit and frontier heads before counting verified completions. A done unit string, ledger label or open-gate count proves no worker completion or authorization. Coordinator-owned bookkeeping may record worker facts; give each worker separate result and receipt paths. Atomic rename is not proof of crash durability or safe adversarial concurrent access.\\n\\n#### The brief\\n\\nYour prompts to agents are your only product, and a sloppy brief compounds into slop across the whole tree. Every spawn carries all of it. A field you cannot fill is a unit you have not scoped yet.\\n\\n```\\nGOAL         one sentence, the outcome, executable by a stranger with no chat access\\nSCOPE        paths this unit may write; paths it may not; its exclusive worktree or branch\\nCONTEXT      pointers to files and PRs; upstream reports pasted in full when this unit\\n             depends on them, because workers cannot see siblings\\nACCEPTANCE   checkable criteria, one per line\\nVERIFY       exact commands or the control-skill path, plus known gotchas\\nTIMEBOX      rough cap on runtime; on expiry, return partial findings and stop rather than run on\\nFORBIDDEN    no gt, no rebase, no force-push, no fixes outside scope, plus unit-specific bans\\nREPORT       status, branch, head SHA, PRs, verdict, what you actually ran, deviations,\\n             suggested follow-ups\\nSTANDING     <preferences.md pasted verbatim>\\n```\\n\\nSize the brief to the unit. A one-command unit gets the template collapsed to a paragraph that still names goal, scope, the verify command, and the report shape. A 4KB scaffold around a two-line edit costs more to write and obey than the edit. A verified readable local standing-orders path may supply the exact text. Paste it verbatim for remote spawns and every resume. Never replace it with a summary.\\n\\nA sub-coordinator brief adds its track boundary and unit list, its spawn budget with supported remote placement and the local exception list, the drain protocol, and the rollup format (per child: name, status, PR, head SHA, verdict, one line, plus track status and frontier delta).\\n\\nA dependency is a context relay, not just ordering. Undeclared upstream context makes the worker guess. Missing fields are a refuse-to-spawn condition. Audit one sampled worker brief per sub-coordinator per wave, concurrently with the wave it samples, never as a gate in front of it. A failing brief stops that track and fixes the sub-coordinator's instructions, not just the worker, because brief quality decays late in a run. Never resume-chain a brief. Respawn fresh with consolidated scope.\\n\\n#### Steps\\n\\n1. **Frame.** State the done predicate as something countable (\\\"all 126 units merged, each verified at each current full head SHA with `unit-test-verified` or better\\\"). Quantify scope: units, rough effort, expected stacks, and the wall-clock budget. If one agent could finish inside that budget, stop here and run Autonomous run instead. Collapsing must not depend on another document being present. It means do the work directly in this session, plain workers where they help, verification inline, landing as you go, and none of the store, register, or pilot machinery below. Schedule landing against the budget. By roughly 70% of it, stop spawning and land what is verified. Name the tracks per project. A contested decomposition or one-way door goes through the arena skill before the pilot. Present the framing once. Reversible prep proceeds without waiting.\\n2. **Initialize the runtime.** Check the helper prerequisites, then run `orch init`. Initialization creates bookkeeping files, not dependencies or workers. Open the trail via the show-me-your-work skill, write the standing orders before any spawn, and seed `frontier.json` from existing PRs with `orch frontier set --repo <repo-dir>`.\\n3. **Pilot.** Push one unit through the whole path: brief, worker, verification, stack entry, ledger row, merge. The pilot exists to falsify the brief template, the verify recipe, and the unit size while that costs one agent instead of fifty. Fix the contract from pilot evidence before any fan-out. Scale the pilot to the unit. On programs of near-identical cheap units, the first unit is the pilot, run as a normal unit with its verify command inline, and fan-out starts the moment it lands. The dedicated pilot pipeline (separate verifier agent, audit gate) is for expensive or novel unit shapes, not for clone-units where a serialized pilot has nothing to falsify.\\n4. **Scale.** Spawn a rolling window of workers up to the in-flight cap, refilling as children finish. Blocking batches pay the slowest child of every batch. Spawn track sub-coordinators only past the one-drain threshold in Roles. Recompute ready work after each drain. Relay upstream reports into downstream briefs. Keep sibling communication upward only. The sampled brief audit runs alongside the wave it samples and stops the next refill on failure, not the current one.\\n5. **Drain.** Run the queue discipline below at every drain point.\\n6. **Land.** Landing is continuous, never a terminal phase. Integration starts with the first verified unit and runs alongside the remaining waves. On heavy repos the stacker is a standing role from wave one, integrating as units verify. On repos where local git is cheap, the coordinator lands verified units itself per Roles. Keep the frontier green before upper-stack work. Stack safety governs. Advance `frontier.json` only on merge or reported new head SHAs.\\n7. **Close.** Drain the final inbox, reconcile every spawned agent to a terminal row (done, abandoned, zombie-reconciled), confirm the predicate on the real artifact, confirm every landed PR has a verdict for its current head SHA, audit the trail per show-me-your-work including its cross-model review, encode recurring corrections into `preferences.md` or the brief template. Leave the store intact. It is the postmortem.\\n\\n#### Queue and drain\\n\\n- On a completion notification, run `orch inbox push <agent> <unit> <status> [--report PATH]` and return to what you were doing. Never deep-review inline. A completion that needs review becomes a verifier unit. Never review a diff inside a drain.\\n- Drain in batches at four points: the end of a critical section, a track rollup, a frontier watcher wake (arm it through an exposed authorized wake binding, with a long heartbeat fallback when explicitly authorized), and before a human report. Begin each batch with the peek-and-receipt protocol above, then `orch inbox drain --json`. Preserve completion notifications that arrive during drain for the next batch. A concurrent helper writer can fail on the store lock; keep that pointer externally and enqueue it at the next safe boundary instead of treating it as saved.\\n- Critical sections you finish first: authoring a brief, a stack operation, a conflict decision, writing a gate, updating ledger or frontier.\\n- Each drain classifies every pointer (landed, needs-verify, failed, zombie, noise), writes the resulting rows through `orch unit add`, `orch unit set`, and `orch ledger record`, runs `orch status`, then spawns the next wave in one message.\\n- Account for every spawned child at its track's rollup: arrived, respawned, or its scope explicitly absorbed. Silently redoing a missing child's work hides both the wasted spend and the coverage gap its result existed to close.\\n- A drain turn ends with the three lines from `orch status`: counts against the states, what changed, gates open. Detail lives in `status.md`. The full reply contract applies at checkpoints and close.\\n\\n#### Stack safety\\n\\n- The frontier is a computed object, never narrative. Recompute `frontier.json` from `gt` after every merge and stack mutation because GitHub base refs drift mid-restack while gt tracking is authoritative: ordered PR list, branch names, head SHAs, a generation number, the lowest unmerged PR. Resolve it where gt knows the stack, normally the stacker's clone. A checkout whose gt metadata never saw the submits reports no PRs and the command errors rather than guessing.\\n- Exactly one stacker per stack may run `gt`, serialized within its stack. Record the holder in the standing orders. Prefer remote restacks when supported and authorized. Missing remote placement remains a limit; never silently substitute local heavy work.\\n- Workers never rebase and never run `gt`. Babysitters follow `playbooks/babysit.md`, one per stack, scoped to one immutable frontier generation. They report conflicts to the stacker rather than restacking.\\n- PR closes and retargets go through the stacker only. Closing a base PR orphans every chain above it. Merges and stack surgery are units with briefs like any other.\\n- One retro watcher follows merged PRs for reverts, post-merge CI breaks, and orphaned follow-ups.\\n\\n#### Verification\\n\\nScale verification to the unit. When VERIFY is a single cheap command, the worker runs it and reports the output, and the coordinator spot-checks receipts. A dedicated verifier agent (on a different model family than the worker) is for units whose verification is expensive, judgment-laden, or high-blast-radius. A verifier agent whose entire product would be rerunning one command is ceremony, not verification.\\n\\nWrite ledger rows with `orch ledger record`. Check the current PR and head SHA with `orch ledger check`. `ledger.tsv`, one row per verdict, keyed by PR number plus head SHA: `live-ui-verified | unit-test-verified | type-check-only | verifier-blocked | verifier-failed`. CI green is an input to a verdict, not a verdict. Behavioral work needs better than `type-check-only`. `verifier-blocked` is not a pass. Respawn when the environment heals. `verifier-failed` gets a fix unit, not a re-verify. A worker may self-report. The coordinator inspects the method evidence and lets a verifier override it on the same key. The helper replaces a row on that key regardless of role; callers must enforce precedence and preserve attempt history separately. A new head SHA voids the row, so re-verify after restack. The ledger answers \\\"was this verified\\\", not memory and not the transcript.\\n\\nA unit is not done until its output is externalized the moment it lands, never batched to the end of the run. An authorized worker pushes its branch, the designated ledger owner records the inspected verifier result, and receipts land in the store. Without push permission, externalization is limited to the authorized durable artifact and landing remains pending. Work that exists only on one VM when that VM dies was never done.\\n\\n#### Liveness and failure\\n\\n- Never resume an agent to check on it. A follow-up can restart an idle worker; inspect its actual state before use. Probe read-only: the ledger, `units.tsv`, `gh`, pushed branches, the worker's observed status through the exposed inspection binding. Transcript mtime is not liveness.\\n- A silent death gets a synthetic postmortem row in the inbox (unit, failure mode, last evidence, options). Replan on evidence as it arrives. Never wait for full quiescence.\\n- Retry by mode: cap-hit or oom, respawn with smaller scope. Network-drop, retry as-is. Tool-error, retry on a different model. Unknown, retry once. Two retries, then abandon the unit and replan around it. Missing required SHA or method evidence uses the stricter assignment limit of one inspected retry. A changed scope or input revision gets a new assignment ID linked to its predecessor. Never retry denied permissions or unknown external outcomes into success.\\n- A zombie that returns hours late reconciles against the current frontier and ledger before anything is accepted. Salvage unique findings through a fresh unit, never a blind merge.\\n- When continued spawning would produce garbage tree-wide (bad upstream output, broken acceptance, dead infra), write a stop line at the top of the standing orders, let in-flight work finish, fix the cause, clear it.\\n- Bound your own infra retries the same way you bound a child's. After a few consecutive tool aborts, stop retrying. Write a terminal handoff to durable state (what is done, where it lives, the exact command to resume) and end the run.\\n- After a host restart, inspect worker state. Do not infer that local agents died or remote work survived. Re-read the standing orders and `units.tsv`, recompute the frontier, reattach surviving remote work by inspected PR and branch plus immutable assignment identity rather than stale agent id, respawn one sub-coordinator per track from its stored brief plus current state, drain, resume. A dead holder does not authorize takeover. `orch` rejects a stale lock until ownership review. Use `--force` only after explicit reviewed takeover permission; it can also steal a live lock.\\n\\n#### Escalation\\n\\nReaches the human, batched into the status page rather than per item: irreversible actions (force-push to shared branches, deploys, deletions, closing someone else's PR), genuine product or preference calls no experiment settles, a standing order that contradicts observed reality, a program-level dead end that survived a replan. Park each as a `gates.md` entry before asking, and route work around it.\\n\\nNever reaches the human: frontier nudges, restack mechanics, retries, CI flake triage, review-thread triage, format fixes, scope the brief already forbids (refuse and continue), and \\\"should I keep going\\\". Act and log within existing authorization. Park any missing permission instead of assuming it.\\n\\nMid-run discoveries fix only what blocks the frontier. Everything else parks in follow-ups. At this fan-out a small scope leak multiplies into PRs nobody asked for.\\n\\n**Reply:** at checkpoints and close: the predicate and the count against it from `units.tsv` and `ledger.tsv`, tracks and what each landed, the frontier (PR list plus SHAs), verdicts summary, what was abandoned and why, gates awaiting the human (the only asks), the store path, and the trail path. Numbers from the tables, not narrative. Include PR links.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"9839239c6ebc72de8165f8a6fa4a6326fea7293e15a47d24f98396df2a61b3fb\",\n    \"bytes\": 1221,\n    \"text\": \"# Poteto agent prompt\\n\\nUse this role for `/poteto-mode` and requests for poteto's style. Supply these instructions to a fresh independent worker for each new task when the session exposes and permits that operation. Resume a worker only in the strict cases named by poteto-mode's Subagents section. Generic worker routing is acceptable only when it carries this entire prompt and the required mode read; otherwise it skips the style contract.\\n\\nBefore any work, read [poteto-mode's SKILL.md](../../skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md) in full, including its inline Principles index. Navigate to the leaf `principle-*` skill whenever you apply that principle. If the mode or an applicable leaf is unavailable, report that missing dependency and stop dependent work. Do not reconstruct the mode from a summary.\\n\\nFollow [model roles](../model-roles.md) and [session capabilities](../host-capabilities.md). This prompt does not register an agent or promise background execution. Record requested and observed models separately. Preserve user authorization and host permissions throughout the mode. An authorized sequential fallback uses the same full read and discloses reduced capability; it does not count as an independent worker.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"plugin.json\",\n    \"sha256\": \"4eabad82a990169c71a1b1e1b6fd2d4b08800af353e653b85ea65a4681651e7b\",\n    \"bytes\": 783,\n    \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"$schema\\\": \\\"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/plugin.schema.json\\\",\\n  \\\"author\\\": {\\n    \\\"email\\\": \\\"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\\\",\\n    \\\"name\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"url\\\": \\\"https://x.com/poteto\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"description\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n  \\\"extensions\\\": {\\n    \\\"com.openai\\\": {\\n      \\\"interface\\\": {\\n        \\\"developerName\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n        \\\"displayName\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n        \\\"logo\\\": \\\"./assets/logo.png\\\",\\n        \\\"shortDescription\\\": \\\"pstack engineering workflows\\\",\\n        \\\"websiteURL\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\"\\n      }\\n    }\\n  },\\n  \\\"homepage\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n  \\\"license\\\": \\\"MIT\\\",\\n  \\\"name\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n  \\\"version\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\"\\n}\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \".codex-plugin/plugin.json\",\n    \"sha256\": \"8f8648553473a22102a0e18fc9641b5348438cc95a23af62a4b8eed864265661\",\n    \"bytes\": 840,\n    \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"interface\\\": {\\n    \\\"developerName\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"displayName\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n    \\\"logo\\\": \\\"./assets/logo.png\\\",\\n    \\\"shortDescription\\\": \\\"pstack engineering workflows\\\",\\n    \\\"websiteURL\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n    \\\"longDescription\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n    \\\"category\\\": \\\"Other\\\",\\n    \\\"capabilities\\\": [],\\n    \\\"defaultPrompt\\\": null\\n  },\\n  \\\"name\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n  \\\"version\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\",\\n  \\\"description\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n  \\\"author\\\": {\\n    \\\"name\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"email\\\": \\\"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\\\",\\n    \\\"url\\\": \\\"https://x.com/poteto\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"keywords\\\": [],\\n  \\\"homepage\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n  \\\"license\\\": \\\"MIT\\\",\\n  \\\"skills\\\": \\\"./skills\\\"\\n}\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/host-capabilities.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20\",\n    \"bytes\": 7155,\n    \"text\": \"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/model-roles.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1\",\n    \"bytes\": 2869,\n    \"text\": \"# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/orchestration-contract.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"5dd740ad66d992df252754d189d0dc207900023372392a0db4309b85eb5e4ec5\",\n    \"bytes\": 6273,\n    \"text\": \"# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/workflow-ownership.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c\",\n    \"bytes\": 1265,\n    \"text\": \"# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/setup/chatgpt.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"4c656ee873b5182e975bbf232988220440c5107db9a1b996185bcd56a4019753\",\n    \"bytes\": 2864,\n    \"text\": \"# Personal ChatGPT setup\\n\\nStarting state for `chatgpt-setup-from-readme` is an ordinary personal ChatGPT conversation with no observed pstack loading. The personal route is unsupported here and explicitly blocked. Do not turn a local filesystem path into a universal ChatGPT installation step.\\n\\nOfficial guidance describes packaging a skills-only plugin, installing it from a local source and testing in a new enabled conversation. It does not establish that this personal conversation exposes that route. Checked 2026-10-08. [Official complete-plugin testing](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/deploy/connect-chatgpt).\\n\\n1. From [README](../../README.md), open this guide and [compatibility](../compatibility.md). Inspect actual plugin controls and tools read-only.\\n2. Record missing or denied loading and discovery operations. Stop the setup branch if the route is absent, or if installation, enabling or settings are not explicitly authorized. Do not add an MCP service to make this skills-only package appear supported.\\n3. For an authorized explanation, supply the exact relevant skill and its required references in the conversation. Follow [workflow selection](../workflows/choosing-a-workflow.md). Label this supplied-source use reduced capability. It is not installed package discovery, persistent activation, independent execution or a host pass.\\n4. If a supported route is later exposed and separately authorized, use the actual host controls and a fresh chat. Record `discover-package`, `enumerate-skills` and `inspect-installed-copy` with observed tool identifiers and artifact evidence. Require all 51 root names, exact installed-copy identity, package version and final frozen SHA, plus the three canonical nested Benny exclusions. Read [inventory](../parity/inventory.json) and [catalog](../skills/README.md) as comparison data only.\\n5. Run the [six walkthrough cases](../parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json) and record actual outputs under the [host contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Workers, execution, persistence, models and integrations need separate exposed and successful bindings. A conversation without them leaves dependent gates blocked.\\n\\nExpected visible result today is an explicit setup/discovery block and a bounded explanation if source is supplied. A future complete installed receipt can change that status only after fresh observations. The [Codex guide](codex.md) describes a different documented route. Its local path and cache rules do not establish personal ChatGPT support.\\n\\n[Optional development dependencies](../testing/helper-setup.md) are separate from host installation. Refresh or removal requires an observed host control and exact authorization; neither source edits nor removing supplied text proves uninstall.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/setup/codex.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"29386cd7894bb56a36ae1285fa005f3794050a017e6eb07000440a9dd80c42f8\",\n    \"bytes\": 3373,\n    \"text\": \"# Codex setup\\n\\nStarting state for `codex-setup-from-readme` is a fresh chat without approved pstack loading. This route is documented, unverified for this package and blocked until exact setup authorization and fresh discovery evidence exist.\\n\\nCurrent official guidance describes local marketplace installation. A repo marketplace uses `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` and a relative `source.path` inside its marketplace root. Restart the Codex desktop app, select that source in the Plugins Directory, install, then start a new enabled chat. The host loads a cached installed copy. Refresh can replace that copy; disabling does not remove it. Follow the current removal control and inspect its result before claiming removal. Source edits alone do not refresh a chat. This saved source review covers the Codex desktop Plugins Directory. Other clients remain unverified. These are documentation facts, not observed access in this account. Checked 2026-10-08. [Official packaging](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/plugins).\\n\\n1. From [README](../../README.md), open this guide and [compatibility](../compatibility.md). Record the candidate version from [plugin.json](../../plugin.json) and full final frozen Git SHA.\\n2. Inspect the host's actual plugin controls read-only. If no local source or plugin control exists, record blocked. Do not silently copy individual skills or change personal configuration to claim package loading.\\n3. Before any setup, obtain explicit authorization for the exact package copy, marketplace file, cache and settings changes, app restart, install and enable scope. This guide is not that authorization. No marketplace is created by this package.\\n4. Only within that approved scope, follow the documented local route above. Verify the installed copy through the actual host operation, not a direct repository read. Start a fresh chat after loading.\\n5. Observe `discover-package`, `enumerate-skills` and `inspect-installed-copy`. Retain the tool identifiers and evidence artifacts. Compare every root name to the [51-skill catalog](../skills/README.md). Exclude the three nested Benny skill paths in [inventory](../parity/inventory.json). Record the actual installed identity, version and exact frozen SHA in a discovery receipt.\\n6. Run the representative [walkthrough cases](../parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json) with [session bindings](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Independent workers need spawn and inspected results. Unknown models remain unknown. Complete direct discovery can pass without workers. A fallback omitting a task requirement is reduced capability and still cannot weaken installed-copy identity or enumeration.\\n\\nUse the [helper guide](../testing/helper-setup.md) only for separately authorized development validation. Missing dependencies block helper-backed work. No dependency install, integration, MCP, lifecycle hook or model persistence is needed merely to read the catalog.\\n\\nExpected visible result is a host-observed installed package with all 51 roots and exact identity, followed by inspected walkthrough artifacts. Without it, loading and discovery remain blocked or not-run. Supplied-source explanations are reduced capability and prove no registration. [Test matrix](../testing/host-matrix.md) defines later host evidence; [troubleshooting](../troubleshooting.md) bounds failures.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/compatibility.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"085c37d688a912c023ff155e0020945fddd7d436f20cf4cd3197a17a5835c01f\",\n    \"bytes\": 3062,\n    \"text\": \"# Candidate compatibility\\n\\nThis is an evidence-bounded candidate summary, not a list of assumed product tools. Current [inventory](parity/inventory.json) records both hosts as not-run. Installed discovery is absent and required host validation is pending. Local source inspection, schema checks and helper fixtures prove no host loading or behavioral parity.\\n\\n| Host or operation | Candidate status | Evidence needed |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Personal ChatGPT loading | Unsupported route, explicitly blocked | Actual supported loading control, exact separate setup authorization and fresh complete discovery |\\n| Codex loading | Documented route, unverified and undiscovered | Actual installed-copy operation, full 51-root enumeration, version and final frozen SHA |\\n| Independent Arena or Swarm | Not-run on both hosts | Authorized observed spawn, terminal results and inspected artifacts |\\n| Sequential supplied-source work | Possible reduced branch, not a recorded pass | Actual authorized reads and artifacts, required observations and complete discovery for package acceptance |\\n| Dependency-backed helpers | Setup-required in operative candidate | Exact local dependency metadata and caller-specific execution evidence |\\n| Historical helper validation | Passed only in isolated snapshot | [Helper guide](testing/helper-setup.md) identifies the exact tested source SHA and limits |\\n| Optional integrations and Benny | Not-run or blocked when unavailable | [Benny readiness](testing/benny-readiness.md), actual bindings and separate action permission |\\n\\nUse [host matrix](testing/host-matrix.md), [case registry](parity/case-registry.json), [case schema](parity/cases/schema.json) and [evidence schema](parity/evidence.schema.json). Required cases need actual host observations or explicit blocked records. Complete discovery needs the identical 51-root receipt in a direct pass, worker pass or validated sequential reduced-pass. Ordinary direct completion is full when it satisfies every case requirement. A reduction never proves worker independence or full orchestration.\\n\\n[Release report](../tools/release_report.py) checks actual root evidence and discovery. An empty evidence directory is not a pass. Regenerate or check this summary against the actual release report after host testing; retain missing, stale and blocked results.\\n\\nFreeze operative content and navigation together. Record the exact tested SHA separately from later report commits in `docs/parity/tested-package.json` only once the frozen SHA exists. The [marker contract](maintenance/rollback.md) permits only explicit narrow report paths and verification changes. Full tracked bytes and modes, including setup and navigation, must match. After blocked records merge and catalog regeneration changes operative catalog bytes, take an explicit second freeze and attach fresh blocked discovery observations to its exact SHA. Preserve both freeze SHAs and earlier records in Git history. The final handoff navigation must be a no-op unless explicitly refrozen; no evidence pass is inherited.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/testing/helper-setup.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"124697022f3133a225be940cd1c6e5d832d65f12ddef70689567d9717cec216e\",\n    \"bytes\": 5369,\n    \"text\": \"# Helper dependency setup\\n\\nBootstrap checks the local [manifest](../../skills/poteto-mode/scripts/package.json), [lock](../../skills/poteto-mode/scripts/bun.lock) and installed package metadata. Missing, unreadable or mismatched files cause a setup-required error and nonzero exit. [ensureDependenciesInstalled](../../skills/poteto-mode/scripts/bootstrap.ts) never installs, spawns, restarts or writes. Use Bun with runtime auto-install disabled.\\n\\n## Exact historical approved scope\\n\\nThe separately approved validation copy was the complete committed snapshot `37b68c8e842b275f310ead112c0ecb7fa0ade308` at `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai-helper-verification`. It was created only after verifying that directory was absent. Its install working directory was `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai-helper-verification/skills/poteto-mode/scripts`. Its cache was `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai-helper-verification/.bun-cache`.\\n\\nThe pins are commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2. Reconciliation changed only the two workspace devDependency requests from latest to the existing exact pins. All 25 package records, transitive requests, platform conditions and integrity values remained byte-identical. These include @types/node 26.1.2, undici-types 8.3.0 and TypeScript platform packages 7.0.2. The operative lock now includes the reviewed request reconciliation. It has SHA-256 `530b7fb6cba7f0b2a6cfc862dbcfe9020c333d45e7e0f448133006583ffdc1f2`. The original lock hash was `667d9cf7222e9aae3fa42a4be383e657784216e9df115da02227fbc5ac7bbe46`.\\n\\n## Procedure for a separately authorized copy\\n\\nNo generic new setup authorization follows from that historical approval. Before future setup, agree an exact immutable source SHA, complete writable copy, absent destination, scripts cwd, task-owned cache and allowed writes. Do not install in a host cache or operative invocation. Use existing Bun. Missing Bun or unavailable exact pins stops the procedure. No substitutes or material resolution/integrity changes are allowed without new review.\\n\\nA reconciliation install, only if specifically needed and authorized, must use `bun install --ignore-scripts --registry=https://registry.npmjs.org --cache-dir=<approved-task-cache>` from the approved scripts cwd. Review any manifest/lock diff before frozen verification. Preserve every direct pin and all 25 transitive/integrity records. The current operative lock already has reconciled requests, so ordinary execution does not regenerate it.\\n\\nFor the historically approved snapshot, the actual frozen install command was:\\n\\n```sh\\nbun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts --registry=https://registry.npmjs.org --cache-dir=/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai-helper-verification/.bun-cache\\n```\\n\\nIt ran from the exact scripts cwd above. It contacted the explicit registry, downloaded packages and wrote snapshot node_modules and its cache. Lifecycle scripts were disabled. No global settings/cache, credentials or operative source writes were approved. A future exact-copy approval must specify its own scope rather than reuse these paths or overwrite this snapshot.\\n\\n## Historical observed validation\\n\\nBun 1.4.2 revision `50a8a838737a8f0e226d1c115788aa18192a2f18` completed the reviewed frozen install. These commands then ran from that snapshot's scripts cwd:\\n\\n```sh\\nbun --no-install -e 'import {ensureDependenciesInstalled} from \\\"./bootstrap.ts\\\"; ensureDependenciesInstalled()'\\nORCH_TEST_ROOT=/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai-helper-test-scratch bun --no-install test bootstrap.test.ts orch watch-pr\\nbun --no-install node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --project watch-pr/tsconfig.json --noEmit --strict\\n```\\n\\nBootstrap passed. The suites passed 85 tests with 316 assertions and zero failures across five files. Strict TypeScript exited 0 without diagnostics. Tests used inert bootstrap spies, injected watcher transports, fake Graphite and owned fixture repositories. No live gh ran. All 299 tracked snapshot files matched the source commit except the reviewed two-request lock reconciliation. Tests and installation preserved the reviewed lock bytes.\\n\\nThe operative candidate has no installed dependencies. Snapshot results support only that source snapshot, not this caller, the current package or either host. Missing current metadata leaves dependency-backed helpers blocked. Development dependency setup is separate from plugin loading, enabling, integration, credentials and host behavior. See [Codex setup](../setup/codex.md), [ChatGPT setup](../setup/chatgpt.md) and [compatibility](../compatibility.md).\\n\\n## Offline maintenance source input\\n\\nSource-inventory tests need the exact canonical subtree as local data. Set `PSTACK_SOURCE_FIXTURE` to its absolute directory when it is outside the previously retained research tree beneath your home Herd directory. Tests verify its 164 paths and recorded per-file hashes against the pinned source; they never fetch or execute it. A missing fixture is an explicit test prerequisite failure. Run maintenance tests through Bun with Python bytecode disabled:\\n\\n```sh\\nPSTACK_SOURCE_FIXTURE=/absolute/path/to/pinned/pstack bun --no-install -e 'const r=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"-m\\\",\\\"unittest\\\",\\\"discover\\\",\\\"-s\\\",\\\"tests\\\",\\\"-v\\\"],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"},stdout:\\\"inherit\\\",stderr:\\\"inherit\\\"});process.exit(r.exitCode)'\\n```\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  }\n]"

      "text": "export function calculate(rows){return rows.reduce((n,r)=>n+r.qty*r.unitPrice,0)}\nexport function adapter(invoice){return calculate(invoice.rows)}\nexport function facade(invoice){return adapter(invoice)}\nexport function controller(invoice){return {total:facade(invoice)}}\nexport function format(n){if(!Number.isFinite(n))throw new Error(\"nonfinite\");return n.toFixed(2)}\n"

Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/arena/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/automate-me\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:automate-me\",\n      \"description\": \"Use for \\\"automate me\\\", \\\"create/update/refresh my -mode skill\\\", \\\"turn/capture my preferences or working style into a skill\\\", or wanting agents to follow how the user works. Drafts or revises a personal -mode skill via create-skill + unslop, optionally pulling fresh evidence from recent transcripts.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/automate-me/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/benchmark-checklist\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:benchmark-checklist\",\n      \"description\": \"Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use for 'blast radius of X', 'what could this break', or reviewing a small diff you don't trust.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/blast-radius/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/bro\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:bro\",\n      \"description\": \"Restate the last message in plain human language, with no jargon.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/bro/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/correct\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:correct\",\n      \"description\": \"Find the mistakes agents keep repeating in this repo and make each one impossible. Try architecture first, then types, then a lint whose error names the fix, then a test, and write docs last. Prove each check fails on a real past mistake. Repeat this each time the operator corrects you. Use for /correct.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/correct/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/create-verification-skill\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:create-verification-skill\",\n      \"description\": \"Generate a project-local verification skill that drives your app the way a user does, any language, framework, or platform. Use for /create-verification-skill, \\\"make a control skill for this repo\\\", or when a project has no scripted way to prove UI/CLI/service behavior.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/create-verification-skill/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/figure-it-out\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:figure-it-out\",\n      \"description\": \"Design an auditable playbook when no narrower one fits: a large migration, an ambitious multi-part change, or work a human reviews after stepping away. Scales rigor to the task, runs a hypothesis loop, and logs decisions via show-me-your-work. Use for /figure-it-out, 'figure it out', a large migration, or when no narrower playbook applies.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/figure-it-out/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/how\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:how\",\n      \"description\": \"Use for \\\"how does X work\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\"where should this live\\\", \\\"which package owns this\\\", \\\"is this the right layer\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/how/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/interrogate\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:interrogate\",\n      \"description\": \"Use for \\\"interrogate\\\", \\\"adversarial review\\\", \\\"multi-model review\\\", \\\"challenge this\\\", \\\"stress test this code\\\", \\\"find blind spots\\\", or \\\"tear this apart\\\". Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/interrogate/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/maintain-verification-skill\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:maintain-verification-skill\",\n      \"description\": \"Periodic pass that keeps a project's verification skill and feature map honest: parallel source readers per feature, one live session driving every feature, at most one PR of proven corrections. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-attack-the-premise/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-boundary-discipline\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-boundary-discipline\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-experience-first/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-explain-the-number\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-explain-the-number\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-fix-root-causes\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-fix-root-causes\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-fix-root-causes/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-never-block-on-the-human\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-never-block-on-the-human\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work.
